## Decisions, 2026-09-09

Joshua answered the three open questions.

1. **Pause stays, and the copy tells the truth.** Not a code change. The six statements say what a pause actually does.
2. **Percentages are allowed, per stimulus and at section level.** Step 7 keeps the section figure. Say it identically on the door and the card, and rename "Recalled" to "Recorded" so the number means what it says.
3. **The runner's LawHub mirror is sanctioned.** Step 11 records `.ac-section` in DESIGN.md and the surface brief as a deliberate mirror, with the Outfit question lock noted as an exception, and tokenises the 13 raw slate hex literals as `--sec-*`.

=== STAGE 440-560
            /*
             * The beat before the first stimulus.
             *
             * The stimulus is deliberately not behind this: it has not started
             * yet, and a section that opened with its first argument already
             * readable would be a section she had begun reading before the
             * recording was worth anything.
             */
            <div className="ac-section-ready" data-mic={micRefused ? "refused" : "live"}>
              {micRefused ? (
                /*
                 * The heading is the fault, not a claim the screen cannot back.
                 *
                 * It said "Recording now" while the footer said nothing was
                 * being recorded, and then counted down into twenty-five
                 * minutes of spoken work that the grader refuses for having no
                 * recording. The consequence is the thing she needs, so the
                 * consequence is what it says, and the two ways forward are
                 * both here rather than neither.
                 */
                <>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-title">No microphone.</p>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                    This drill is spoken all the way through, so without one there is nothing to
                    mark at the end. Allow the microphone for this site in your browser, then ask
                    again. Nothing has started, and your time has not begun.
                  </p>
                  {isEmbedded() && (
                    <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                      This page is inside a preview window, and a preview window is never given the
                      microphone. Open the drill in its own tab and start it there.
                    </p>
                  )}
                  <div className="ac-section-ready-actions">
                    <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-retry" onClick={onRetryMic}>
                      Ask again
                    </button>
                    {isEmbedded() && (
                      <button
                        type="button"
                        className="ac-section-ready-retry"
                        onClick={() =>
                          window.open(window.location.href, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")
                        }
                      >
                        Open in its own tab
                      </button>
                    )}
                    {onExit && (
                      <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-leave" onClick={onExit}>
                        Leave this section
                      </button>
                    )}
                  </div>
                </>

              ) : (
                <>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-title">Recording now</p>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                    One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus: once when
                    you have read it all, once when you have given The Recall.
                  </p>
                  <span className="ac-section-ready-count">
                    {countdown > 0 ? `Starting in ${countdown}` : "Starting"}
                  </span>
                </>
              )}
            </div>
          ) : (
            <div className="ac-section-body">
              {/*
                While she works, every chunk is rendered in order with nothing
                marking one out from another: the cuts guide her eye and hold the
                grader's Details, and that is all they do. During The Recall the
                stimulus is not rendered at all -- the paper stays, the text goes,
                and it does not come back until the Report Card.
              */}
              {phase === "working" ? (
                <div className="ac-section-stimulus">
                  {stimulus.chunks.map((chunk) => (
                    <span key={chunk.n} className="ac-section-chunk">
                      {chunk.text}{" "}
                    </span>
                  ))}
                </div>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          )}

          {/* The card's own footer, where the real section keeps its controls.
              What she is doing sits left, the one key that does it sits right. */}
          <div className="ac-section-foot">
            <p className="ac-section-instruction" role="status">
              {paused
                ? "Paused. Nothing is being recorded."
                : countdown !== null
                  ? micRefused
                    ? "The section has not started."
                    : "Say something to check your microphone."
                  : INSTRUCTION[phase]}
              {/* No presence bar while she is away. The microphone is still
                  open -- pausing a recorder does not close the input -- so a
                  bar left running would move to an empty room and say the one
                  thing it exists to say when it moves: that this is being
                  heard. */}
              {getStream && !paused ? (
                <VoiceLevel getStream={getStream} phase={phase} onDeadInput={onDeadInput} />
              ) : null}
            </p>
            {/* The fault and the instruction, not the fault INSTEAD of it.
                These were a ternary, so a microphone warning took away the only
                thing on the screen telling her which key advances the drill --
                on precisely the run where something has already gone wrong. The
                warning leads, because it is the new information. */}
            <div className="ac-section-foot-right">
              {/* A pause she asked for and did not get.
                  It belongs in the footer rather than on the paused card
                  precisely BECAUSE she is not paused: the card she would have
                  read it on is the one that never appeared, and a refusal with
                  nowhere to be said is a button that does nothing. */}

=== RUNNER 120-135, 205-260, 405-500
const defaultNow = () => Date.now();

/**
 * How long the ready beat runs before the first stimulus.
 *
 * Long enough to read one line and see the level move, short enough that it is
 * never in the way of a student on her fourth section of the week.
 */
const READY_SECONDS = 3;

/** A row gets a few quiet chances while she is still working, never an unbounded loop. */
const ROW_RETRY_ATTEMPTS = 3;
const ROW_RETRY_BASE_MS = 500;
const ROW_RETRY_MAX_MS = 4_000;

export function SectionRunner({
   * first run does not bring it back. She can also hide it early.
   */
  const [coachOn, setCoachOn] = useState(() => !hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId));
  useEffect(() => {
    if (coachOn) markWalkthroughSeen(studentId);
    // Deliberately once, on mount, against the value read at mount.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, []);
  /**
   * Seconds left in the ready beat, counting to zero, and null once she is
   * working.
   *
   * The microphone opens on mount and the clock used to start with it, so the
   * first thing a 25-minute unrepeatable take captured was a student still
   * arranging herself -- and the very first frame she saw was already stimulus
   * one, with no moment in between that said the recording had begun. Three
   * seconds is the whole fix: the recorder is live through it, so the level
   * indicator is moving and a dead microphone is visible BEFORE any of it
   * costs her anything, and the section's own clock does not start until it
   * ends.
   */
  const [countdown, setCountdown] = useState<number | null>(readySeconds);
  /** Confirming the way out. Leaving throws the whole run away. */
  const [confirmingExit, setConfirmingExit] = useState(false);
  /**
   * Nothing reaching the microphone. Shown rather than logged: it is the one
   * fault that otherwise ends in a perfect-looking run and a card of zeros.
   *
   * Two-way, and it was not. `setDeadInput(true)` was the only call in the
   * tree, so the warning could be raised and never lowered: one skipped Recall
   * put "Nothing is reaching the microphone" on the screen at 121s and left it
   * there for the remaining twenty-five stimuli, through four minutes of speech
   * the drill heard perfectly well. `VoiceLevel` now reports the state rather
   * than announcing the fault, and this is the setter for both directions.
   */
  const [deadInput, setDeadInput] = useState(false);
  /** The recording did not reach the server. Said plainly at the end. */
  const [uploadFailed, setUploadFailed] = useState(false);
  /** Response rows still absent after the per-row end-of-run backstop. */
  const [unsavedCount, setUnsavedCount] = useState(0);
  /** True only after a real recording has reached storage. */
  const [recordingStored, setRecordingStored] = useState(false);
  /**
   * This sitting's own name for its local backup.
   *
   * Made before anything else, because pieces of the recording start arriving
   * long before the Attempt number comes back, and every piece has to be
   * filed under something. The run record that says where the pieces belong is
   * written as soon as the Attempt is known.
   */
  /**
   * She stepped away. The section is still open and its clock is not running.
   *
   * A section is twenty-five unbroken minutes and is scored whole, which used to
   * mean the only way out of one was the way that throws it away. So a student
   * who had to answer the door had two options and both were bad: sit through
   * only thing in this component that re-renders on a timer: nothing else on
   * screen changes between her presses, and the recorder is untouched by it --
   * `mark()` reads refs and the recorder is started once on mount.
   */
  const [elapsed, setElapsed] = useState(0);

  /*
   * The ready beat, and the moment the section actually starts.
   *
   * Every clock in the run is re-zeroed as it ends, so the three seconds are
   * not charged to her goal. The RECORDER is deliberately not restarted: it
   * has been running since mount, which is what lets her see the level move
   * before the first stimulus, and restarting a recorder mid-section is the
   * one thing the drill forbids outright. The beat simply sits at the head of
   * the file, inside stimulus one's first window, as three seconds of nothing.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    if (countdown === null) return;
    /*
     * The beat is also the gate.
     *
     * `getUserMedia` is a prompt she has to answer, and answering it can take
     * longer than three seconds, so a beat that ticks regardless would start
     * the section before the microphone had said anything at all. It waits for
     * an answer, and if the answer is no it holds here rather than spending
     * twenty-five unrepeatable minutes on a run nothing can mark.
     */
    if (!recording && !micRefused) return;
    if (micRefused) return;
    if (countdown <= 0) {
      startedAt.current = runNow();
      phaseStartedAt.current = startedAt.current;
      stimulusStartedAt.current = startedAt.current;
      setCountdown(null);
      return;
    }
    const t = setTimeout(() => setCountdown((n) => (n === null ? null : n - 1)), 1000);
    return () => clearTimeout(t);
  }, [countdown, runNow, recording, micRefused]);

  /*
   * `countdown` is a dependency on purpose. This effect returns early during
   * the beat, so the ticker can only start when the beat ends -- and the beat
   * ending is a change to `countdown`. Without it the effect ran once, at
   * mount, and the clock read 0:00 for the whole of every run.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    if (done || countdown !== null || paused) return;
    const tick = setInterval(() => setElapsed(seconds(startedAt.current)), 1000);
    return () => clearInterval(tick);
  }, [countdown, done, paused, seconds]);

  /*
   * Hold the app still for the length of the run.
   *
   * Two things in the browser will end a section without asking. The app's own
   * freshness check reloads the tab when a newer bundle has been published, on
   * every return to it -- so publishing mid-section and her glancing away was
   * enough to lose twenty-five minutes. And a reflex Cmd-R or a closed tab does
   * the same thing by hand.
   *
   * The first is suppressed outright: the reload is deferred until the run
   * ends, which costs her one stale bundle for one section. The second cannot
   * be suppressed, so it is questioned -- `beforeunload` is the browser's own
   * prompt and the only guard available, and it is deliberately registered for
   * the whole run rather than only once she has spoken, because the expensive
   * thing to lose is the recording and that starts here.

  useEffect(() => {
    const release = beginLiveRun();
    const warn = (e: BeforeUnloadEvent) => {
      e.preventDefault();
      // Set for the browsers that still require it; none of them show the
      // string any more, and the prompt is generic by design.
      e.returnValue = "";
    };
    window.addEventListener("beforeunload", warn);
    return () => {
      window.removeEventListener("beforeunload", warn);
      release();

  return (

      phase={cursor.phase}

=== VoiceLevel
import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

/**
 * How long a phase has to run before its silence means anything.
 *
 * A Recall she ends in three seconds carries no evidence either way, and
 * accusing the hardware on that is how a warning stops being believed. A window
 * shorter than this counts neither for the fault nor against it.
 */
const MIN_WINDOW_MS = 8000;

/**
 * Consecutive silent windows before the fault is raised.
 *
 * Both halves of a stimulus are spoken -- "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in
 * as you go", then "Say the whole argument back from memory" -- so a dead input
 * is silent through every window there is. A skip is silent through exactly
 * one: she had nothing for that Recall, and then she reads the next stimulus
 * aloud like all the others.
 *
 * One window was therefore the wrong unit, and it accused the hardware on the
 * most ordinary thing a student does. Two consecutive is the shortest run a skip
 * cannot produce, and it costs a genuinely dead microphone one further stimulus
 * before it is named.
 */
const SILENT_WINDOWS = 2;

/** Full deflection. Chosen so ordinary speech uses most of the bar. */
const FULL_SCALE = 0.15;

export interface VoiceLevelProps {
  /** The live input, or null when the microphone never opened. */
  getStream: () => MediaStream | null;
  phase: SectionPhase;
  /**
   * Whether nothing is reaching the microphone, called only when the answer
   * changes.
   *
   * It used to be a one-way announcement, fired once and never taken back, and
   * the runner had no setter that could clear it. One deliberate skip therefore
   * told a student her hardware was broken, and went on telling her for the
   * remaining twenty-five stimuli and four minutes of clearly heard speech.
   */
  onDeadInput?: (dead: boolean) => void;
}

export function VoiceLevel({ getStream, phase, onDeadInput }: VoiceLevelProps) {
  const bar = useRef<HTMLSpanElement>(null);
  const level = useRef(0);
  /** The loudest thing heard since the current phase began. */
  const peak = useRef(0);
  const windowStartedAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
  /** Consecutive windows that ran long enough to count and carried nothing. */
  const silentWindows = useRef(0);
  /** What the runner has been told, so it is told only when that changes. */
  const dead = useRef(false);
  const onDead = useRef(onDeadInput);
  onDead.current = onDeadInput;

  /*
   * One analyser for the life of the run.
   *
   * Opened once and closed once, like the recorder itself: an AudioContext per
   * phase would be forty-four of them across a section, and Chrome caps how
   * many a page may hold at the same time.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    let raf = 0;
    let context: AudioContext | null = null;
    let stopped = false;

    const attach = () => {
      if (stopped) return;
      const stream = getStream();
      // The recorder opens the microphone asynchronously and this mounts with
      // it, so the stream is usually a moment away rather than absent. Ask
      // again next frame; a genuinely refused microphone simply never arrives
      // and the bar stays flat, which is the truth.
      if (!stream) {
        raf = requestAnimationFrame(attach);
        return;
      }
      const Ctor =
        window.AudioContext ??
        (window as unknown as { webkitAudioContext?: typeof AudioContext }).webkitAudioContext;
      if (!Ctor) return;
      context = new Ctor();
      const analyser = context.createAnalyser();
      // Small window, because this is an amplitude reading and not a spectrum:
      // it costs less per frame and follows her voice more closely.
      analyser.fftSize = 512;
      context.createMediaStreamSource(stream).connect(analyser);
      const samples = new Float32Array(analyser.fftSize);

      const read = () => {
        analyser.getFloatTimeDomainData(samples);
        let sum = 0;
        for (let i = 0; i < samples.length; i++) sum += samples[i] * samples[i];
        const rms = Math.sqrt(sum / samples.length);
        if (rms > peak.current) peak.current = rms;
        /*
         * Recovery is immediate, and deliberately not deferred to the next
         * boundary like the fault is. The fault needs a whole window to be sure;
         * being heard again needs one frame, and a warning that outlives the
         * condition it describes is the thing being fixed. Guarded on `dead` so
         * this is a transition rather than a call at 60Hz.
         */
        if (rms >= NOISE_FLOOR && dead.current) {
          dead.current = false;
          silentWindows.current = 0;
          onDead.current?.(false);
        }
        // Rises fast and falls slowly, so the bar tracks speech rather than
        // flickering on every syllable boundary.
        level.current = rms > level.current ? rms : level.current * 0.88 + rms * 0.12;
        if (bar.current) {
          bar.current.style.transform = `scaleX(${Math.min(1, level.current / FULL_SCALE)})`;
        }
        raf = requestAnimationFrame(read);
      };
      read();
    };

    attach();
    return () => {
      stopped = true;
      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
      void context?.close();
    };
  }, [getStream]);

  /*
   * The fault, decided at the boundary rather than during a phase.
   *
   * Judging it while she is mid-sentence would fire on a long pause. Judging it
   * as a phase closes uses the whole window, which is the only reading that
   * separates "she went quiet" from "nothing is arriving".
   *
   * Every boundary is judged now, not only the end of a Recall. The working
   * phase is spoken too, so its silence is evidence of exactly the same kind,
   * and using both is what lets a run of them mean something a single one
   * cannot.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    const started = windowStartedAt.current;
    const loudest = peak.current;
    peak.current = 0;
    windowStartedAt.current = Date.now();

    // The first run of this effect opens the first window; nothing has closed.
    if (started === null) return;
    // Too short to carry evidence either way, so it neither raises the fault
    // nor clears the count towards it.
    if (Date.now() - started < MIN_WINDOW_MS) return;

    if (loudest >= NOISE_FLOOR) {
      silentWindows.current = 0;
      return;
    }
    silentWindows.current += 1;
    if (silentWindows.current >= SILENT_WINDOWS && !dead.current) {
      dead.current = true;
      onDead.current?.(true);
    }
  }, [phase]);

  return (
    <span className="ac-section-level" aria-hidden>
      <span ref={bar} className="ac-section-level-fill" />
    </span>
  );
}
=== CSS 520-620
  color: #475569;
}

.ac-section-confirm-go:hover {
  border-color: #cbd5e1;
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* ---- the ready beat ----------------------------------------------------- */

/* Three seconds between pressing Start and the first stimulus.
 *
 * The microphone opens on mount and the clock starts with it, so without this
 * the first thing a 25-minute recorded take captures is a student still
 * settling. It also puts the one instruction she needs -- Space, twice per
 * stimulus -- on screen at the moment it becomes true. */
.ac-section-ready {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  flex-direction: column;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 3rem 1.5rem;
  text-align: center;
}

.ac-section-ready-title {
  margin: 0;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 26px;
  font-weight: 400;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
}

.ac-section-ready-body {
  max-width: 34ch;
  margin: 0;
  color: #64748b;
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.65;
}

.ac-section-ready-count {
  /* Was #94a3b8: 2.56:1 at 13px, which is a countdown she cannot read. */
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

/* A break wears the ready beat's clothes on purpose. It is the same kind of
   moment -- the section open, the clock not running, one thing to press -- and
   a second full-card layout for it would be a second thing to keep true. */
.ac-section-ready--paused .ac-section-ready-title {
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* The refused microphone reads as a fault rather than as a beat, so the heading
   takes the warning colour the footer uses for the same class of thing. */
.ac-section-ready[data-mic="refused"] .ac-section-ready-title {
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* Two ways forward, neither of which existed. Asking again leads, because it is
   the one that keeps the section. */
.ac-section-ready-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.75rem;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  justify-content: center;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry,
.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border-radius: 9999px;
  padding: 0.4rem 1.1rem;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease, border-color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-ink);
  background: var(--sec-ink);
  color: var(--sec-paper);
}

.ac-section-ready-retry:hover {
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  border-color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);
  background: none;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

=== level css 425-460, 620-640

/* Presence, not a level meter.
 *
 * The one moving thing on a screen she talks at for twenty-one minutes. It
 * answers "is anything reaching it" and deliberately not "how am I doing":
 * no target, no colour change, no number, because nothing in this room is
 * allowed to read as a verdict before the section ends. Graphite, low
 * contrast, and beside the instruction rather than anywhere near the text.
 */
.ac-section-level {
  display: inline-block;
  width: 34px;
  height: 3px;
  margin-left: 0.625rem;
  overflow: hidden;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: #e2e8f0;
  vertical-align: middle;
}

.ac-section-level-fill {
  display: block;
  height: 100%;
  transform: scaleX(0);
  transform-origin: left center;
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
}

/* ---- leaving ------------------------------------------------------------ */

/* A half-finished section writes nothing by design, so the button that ends one
   is the most expensive control in the product. It asks first, over the run
   rather than instead of it: the stimulus stays behind the scrim, because she
   may well be here by accident and the way back is to see where she was. */
.ac-section-confirm {
  position: fixed;
}

.ac-section-ready-leave:hover {
  background: #f1f5f9;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section-level-fill {
    transition: none;
  }
}


/* ---- leaving the drill room -------------------------------------------- */

/* The one composed moment in the drill.
 *
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is a
 * violet-cast white on a violet ground. The jump between them was abrupt
 * because there was nothing between them: the field cut to the card in one

/**
 * The three seconds before the first stimulus.
 *
 * The microphone opens on mount and the clock used to start with it, so the
 * first thing an unrepeatable 25-minute take captured was a student still
 * settling -- and her first frame was already stimulus one. The beat is where
 * the recording is proved: the level indicator is live through it, so a muted
 * input is visible before anything costs her.
 */
describe("the ready beat", () => {
  it("does not show the first stimulus until the beat has run", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    expect(screen.getByText(/recording now/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(CHUNKS[0].text)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("ignores Space during it, so a stray press cannot start the section early", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    space();
    space();
    space();
    // Still the beat: the presses went nowhere rather than eating stimulus one.
    expect(screen.getByText(/recording now/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("holds the section clock at zero through it", async () => {
    // Three seconds of settling must not be charged to a 25-minute goal.
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    expect(screen.getByText("0:00")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("hands over to the first stimulus when it ends", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 0 });
    expect(await screen.findByText(CHUNKS[0].text)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/recording now/i)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

/**
 * Leaving.
 *
 * A half-finished section writes nothing and is deliberately never a partial
 * score, which is the right rule and is exactly what makes one click on a
 * permanently visible button worth up to twenty-five minutes.
 */
/*
 * The microphone said no, and the drill is only spoken.
 *
 * With `getUserMedia` rejecting, the beat showed the heading "Recording now"
 * over a footer saying nothing was being recorded, counted to zero anyway, and
 * handed her a 26-stimulus run that the grader refuses for having no recording.
 * There was no way to ask again and no way to stop before the clock started.
 */
describe("a refused microphone", () => {
  beforeEach(() => refuseMicrophone());

  it("does not claim to be recording", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    expect(screen.queryByText(/recording now/i)).toBeNull();
    expect(screen.getByText(/no microphone/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("names the consequence rather than the fault", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    expect(screen.getByText(/nothing to mark at the end/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("holds the beat instead of counting into a run nothing can mark", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    expect(screen.queryByText(/starting in/i)).toBeNull();
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 1200));
    // Still the beat, and still no stimulus: the clock has not started.
    expect(screen.getByText(/no microphone/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    for (const chunk of CHUNKS) {
      expect(document.body.textContent).not.toContain(chunk.text);
    }
  }, 10000);

  it("cannot be started past by pressing the key", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    space();
    space();
    expect(screen.getByText(/no microphone/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(upsert).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("offers a way to ask again, and asking again starts the section", async () => {
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3 });
    const again = screen.getByRole("button", { name: /ask again/i });
    grantMicrophone();
    await act(async () => {
      fireEvent.click(again);
    });
    expect(screen.queryByText(/no microphone/i)).toBeNull();
    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(screen.getByText(/recording now/i)).toBeInTheDocument(), {
      timeout: 4000,
    });
  }, 10000);

  it("offers a way out, and leaving costs nothing because nothing has begun", async () => {
    const onExit = vi.fn();
    await renderRun({ readySeconds: 3, onExit });
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /leave this section/i }));
    expect(onExit).toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});

/**
 * A microphone that works, because a section without one no longer runs.
 *
 * jsdom has neither `navigator.mediaDevices` nor `MediaRecorder`, so every test
 * in this file used to take the recorder's refused-microphone path -- which was
 * the wrong default even before it stopped working. The drill is spoken end to
 * end and the grader refuses a run with no recording, so the ready beat now
 * holds rather than counting down into twenty-five minutes nothing can mark.
 * The beat holding without a microphone is asserted on its own, below.
 */
class FakeRecorder {
  static isTypeSupported = () => true;
  ondataavailable: ((e: { data: Blob }) => void) | null = null;
  onstop: (() => void) | null = null;
  state = "inactive";
  constructor(
    _stream: MediaStream,
    readonly options?: { mimeType?: string },
  ) {}
  get mimeType() {
    return this.options?.mimeType ?? "audio/webm";
  }
  start() {
    this.state = "recording";
  }
  stop() {
    this.state = "inactive";
    this.ondataavailable?.({ data: new Blob(["x"], { type: this.mimeType }) });
    this.onstop?.();
  }
}

/** Granted, unless a test says otherwise. */
function grantMicrophone() {
  const stream = { getTracks: () => [{ stop: () => {} }] } as unknown as MediaStream;
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.resolve(stream)) },
  });
}

/** Refused, the way a browser refuses it. */
function refuseMicrophone() {
  vi.stubGlobal("MediaRecorder", FakeRecorder);
  const denied = Object.assign(new Error("denied"), { name: "NotAllowedError" });
  Object.defineProperty(navigator, "mediaDevices", {
    configurable: true,
    value: { getUserMedia: vi.fn(() => Promise.reject(denied)) },
  });
}

/** One stimulus, so a full run is two presses. */
const ONE = { ...pt105, items: [pt105.items[0]] };
/** Two, for the rules about what never comes back. */
const TWO = { ...pt105, items: pt105.items.slice(0, 2) };
/** The 26-stimulus section observed in the audit. */
const TWENTY_SIX = pt134;
const CHUNKS = ONE.items[0].chunks;

async function renderRun(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionRunner>[0]> = {}) {

    <SectionRunner
      assignmentId="a1"

======
/**
 * A preview harness for the section launch surface. Development only.
 *
 * It exists so the component can be looked at in a real browser, in every state
 * and at every breakpoint, without signing in and without a graded section
 * sitting in the database. Only the network is stubbed: `window.fetch` is
 * replaced before anything imports the Supabase client, so the component, the
 * client helper and the derivation all run exactly as they do in the product.
 *

function stim(item_order: number, over: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  return {
    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    item_order,
    carried: 3,
    total: 3,
    percent: 100,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0,
    spoken: "she said something here",
    ...over,
  };
}

const SLOTS = 25;
function run(seconds: number, createdAt: string, gaps: number[] = [], silent: number[] = []) {
  return {
    assignment_id: `assign-${seconds}`,
    attempt_no: 1,
    source_section: "PT105.S2",
    graded_at: createdAt,
    created_at: createdAt,
    stimuli: Array.from({ length: SLOTS }, (_, i) => {
      const n = i + 1;
      if (silent.includes(n)) return stim(n, { spoken: "", carried: 0, percent: 0 });
      if (gaps.includes(n)) return stim(n, { ungraded: "judge returned nothing" });
      return stim(n);

## assistant

Step 8 is built, tested, and up as [PR #105](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/105). Not merged, since you merged the last two yourself.

**Production answer to the open question.** Both Step 6 and Step 7 are live on lsatprep.study. The bundle moved from `index-Bv9106bQ.js` to `index-CEIwSuho.js`, and the chunks carry the Step 6 markers, the Step 7 "Recorded" and "met on" strings, and the handover ground as `#e9e4ed`, which is the hex form the build gives the oklch value. Three markers from the handoff list came back empty only because minification rewrote them.

**What Step 8 changed**, commit `41fc2e0` on `rrc-step-8`:

- The beat now reads "Microphone on." with the level bar in the panel, and nothing else. The thirty-word Space instruction moved to the briefing foot, which also stops claiming the timer starts the moment she presses Start.
- The count does not begin until the microphone has heard something once, or six seconds pass, whichever is first. Holding shows "Say something to check your microphone." with no number. Heard shows "Heard you." and the count. Expired unheard shows "Nothing heard yet. Check it is not muted." and the count.
- The check's clock starts when the microphone opens, not at mount, so a slow permission prompt cannot eat it. A zero-second beat has no check, which keeps every existing test as it was.
- New harness at `src/dev/section-beat-preview.html?state=holding|heard|unheard|refused|running`, used to verify the three states and the bar placement in the browser.

Suite is 106 files, 2787 tests, `tsc` clean. Plan file has a dated "Step 8: done" section.

**One thing worth knowing.** A polling query for "heard you" finds two elements, because the footer status also says it. The test uses the exact panel string.

**Not done.** Vault wrapup is still outstanding across three sessions. The merged `rrc-step-6` and `rrc-step-7` branches are still there. Step 9, the quieter door, is next.

That's the harness, not the drill. The page you have open is `section-beat-preview.html?state=running`, the dev harness I added this session to look at the ready beat. Its stimulus is a two-chunk fixture I wrote by hand, so the paper shows exactly those two sentences ("The council's own survey contradicts the figure it published. So the figure cannot be the one the report relied on.") and nothing else.

The `running` state exists only to prove the level bar moves back to the rail once the beat ends, which is the small grey line beside "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go." in your screenshot. The real runner renders the full stimulus from the section JSON, and that path is unchanged in this step.

If you want the harness to show a realistic stimulus, I can point it at a real item from the PT105 fixture the tests use instead of the two-sentence stub.

=== attempts
.sl-sec-attempts {
  list-style: none;
  margin: 10px 0 0;
  padding: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 4px;
}
.sl-sec-body > .sl-note {
  margin-top: 10px;
}
/* A past Attempt is the way back to its Report Card, so it is a control and
   not a footnote. It was 13.5px muted text with a dotted underline and no
   padding, which is what a caption looks like, and the student it was built
   for read the score off it and asked where the graded responses were
   (Celia, 2026-09-08). Bordered, full width, and 44px of target. */
.sl-sec-attempt {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--rd-paper, #fff);
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line, rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.12));
  border-radius: 10px;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 13.5px;
  gap: 4px 12px;
  justify-content: space-between;
  min-height: 44px;
  padding: 8px 14px;
  text-align: left;
  transition:
    border-color 160ms ease,
    color 160ms ease;
  width: 100%;
}
.sl-sec-attempt[data-flat="true"] {
  background: none;
  border-style: dashed;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  cursor: default;
}
.sl-sec-attempt:hover:not([data-flat="true"]) {
  border-color: currentColor;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
/* What the row opens, said on the row rather than left to be guessed. */
.sl-sec-attempt-go {
  font-size: 12.5px;
  font-weight: 600;
  white-space: nowrap;
}
.sl-sec-attempt:not([data-flat="true"]) .sl-sec-attempt-go {
  text-decoration: underline;
  text-underline-offset: 3px;
}
.sl-sec-go {
  appearance: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  min-height: 44px;
  padding: 0 22px;

=== head 1-18, 44-50, 95-100
/* The Read, Recall, Colour door.
 *
 * One evening, in two registers. The hero is the runner's own indigo lifted
 * into a dusk sky over a painted meadow, cut into depth planes that move at
 * their own rates as she scrolls; everything under it sits on cream paper in
 * the same light. The method is performed before it is explained, the four
 * steps are the next thing she meets, and the sections she can run sit
 * directly beneath.
 *
 * Every value is scoped to `.sl`. Nothing here touches the runner
 * (`recall/section.css`) or the shell outside this door. Class names beginning
 * `sl-` are the door's contract with its tests and its two wrappers; `rd-`
 * names are the design and may change freely.
 */

.sl {
  /* The hero panel's ink. Named so a rule that borrows it onto cream is
     visible as a mistake instead of reading as an ordinary colour. */

  min-height: 100%;
  background: var(--rd-cream);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-family: var(--rd-sans);
  font-size: 16px;
  line-height: 1.5;

/* The scene: five planes and a stage, each its own layer. */
.mw {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 0;

## user

Tool result:
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

const CSS = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, "section-launch.css"), "utf8");

/**
 * A `var(--name)` whose custom property is never defined does not fail loudly.
 * It falls back, or it inherits, and the result is text nobody can read that no
 * test notices. `--rd-ink-soft` did exactly that here: two rules asked for it,
 * it had never been declared, and the section headings quietly rendered fainter
 * than the body copy underneath them.
 *
 * CLAUDE.md carries the same warning from an earlier round, where three token
 * names shipped undefined for five months and removed the focus ring from every
 * shared Button.
 */
describe("section-launch.css", () => {
  const defined = new Set([...CSS.matchAll(/(--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
  const used = [...CSS.matchAll(/var\((--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);

  it("defines every --rd- token it uses", () => {
    const missing = [...new Set(used)].filter((n) => !defined.has(n));
    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("gives the two buttons their own focus ring, not the world's", () => {
    // `.sl :focus-visible` sets one ring for the whole world and loses the
    // cascade on these two classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible
    // and computed outline-style: none. Measured in the browser, not inferred.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-start:focus-visible");
    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-brief:focus-visible");
    // The door draws both on a dark panel, where an ink ring is invisible.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible");
  });

  it("sways the grass in groups, not once per blade", () => {
    // 170 blades each carrying their own keyframe put 191 animations on the
    // compositor at rest, for a picture that is usually scrolled past.
    expect(CSS).not.toContain(".mw-blades path {");
    expect(CSS).toContain(".mw-blades g {");
    // Skew, not rotation: rotation about a shared origin lifts the far blades
    // off the ground line, and the planted base is the whole look.
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/@keyframes mw-sway\s*\{[^@]*skewX/);
  });

  it("stops every loop when its surface leaves the screen", () => {
    for (const sel of [".mw[data-still]", ".rd-demo[data-still]", ".rd-method[data-still]"]) {
      expect(CSS).toContain(sel);
    }
    expect(CSS).toContain("animation-play-state: paused");
  });

  it("promises the compositor nothing for the layer that never moves", () => {
    const grain = CSS.match(/\.mw-grain\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(grain).toMatch(/will-change:\s*auto/);
  });

  it("gives the demonstration an ink focus ring, not the hero's white one", () => {
    // .rd-hero :focus-visible is white for the dark panel. The demo card is
    // cream paper sitting on it, where white measured 1.05:1.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".rd-hero .rd-demo :focus-visible");
  });

  it("makes the three small controls pressable at 44px", () => {
    // Visual size is unchanged; the target is an overlay, the way the runner's
    // rail controls were done. Measured in the browser: 44x44, 73x47, 58x47.
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/\.rd-demo-play::after\s*\{[^}]*inset:\s*-8px/);
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/\.sl-crumb-back::after\s*\{[^}]*inset:\s*-14px -8px/);
    const tab = CSS.match(/\.sl-dr-tab\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(tab).toMatch(/padding:\s*10px 0 14px/);
  });

  it("keeps the way out above the hero copy", () => {
    // Same layer, and the copy comes later in the DOM, so on a phone it took
    // the bottom half of the crumb's target with it.
    const zIndexOf = (selector: string) => {
      const after = CSS.split(`${selector} {`)[1] ?? "";
      return Number(after.slice(0, after.indexOf("}")).match(/z-index:\s*(\d+)/)?.[1]);
    };
    expect(zIndexOf(".rd-hero-top")).toBeGreaterThan(zIndexOf(".rd-hero-copy"));
  });

  it("draws no text in flat white on the briefing page, which sits on cream", () => {
    // The hero classes are white for the door's dark panel. Any of them reused
    // on .sl-briefing-page must restate a colour.
    const scoped = [...CSS.matchAll(/\.sl-briefing-page\s+\.([a-z0-9-]+)\s*\{([^}]*)\}/g)];
    for (const [, cls, body] of scoped) {
      expect(`${cls}: ${body}`).not.toMatch(new RegExp(String.raw`color:\s*(#fff\b|var\(--rd-on-dark\))`, "i"));
    }
  });
});

=== noKeyboard
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

/**
 * Whether this device probably has no keyboard, so the drill's one key is out
 * of reach.
 *
 * A section advances on Space and on nothing else
 * (`SectionRunner.tsx`, "One key, and only Space"). That is deliberate: no
 * mouse in the loop, so her hands never leave the argument. It also means a
 * phone can open the drill, read stimulus 1, and never get to stimulus 2 --
 * the briefing, the door and the library all render fine at 390x844, so
 * nothing warns her before the recording is already running.
 *
 * `(pointer: coarse)` is the test rather than a width, because the question is
 * "is there a keyboard", not "is the screen small". A narrow desktop window has
 * a keyboard and must keep the pure one-key loop; a large tablet has no
 * keyboard and needs the control. Width answers neither.
 *
 * This is a hint, never a gate. Joshua's call, 2026-09-04: a phone is warned
 * and let through, not refused. So nothing here disables anything -- it adds a
 * sentence at the door and an on-screen way to advance inside the run, and
 * Space stays authoritative everywhere.
 */
export const NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY = "(pointer: coarse)";

/** Read once, outside React, so a non-browser render has a defined answer. */
export function hasNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return false;
  return window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY).matches;
}

/**
 * The same question as a hook, kept live.
 *
 * A tablet with a keyboard case attached or removed mid-run changes the answer,
 * and the control appearing or going away is the correct response to that. The
 * initial state is computed rather than defaulted to `false`, so the first
 * paint on a phone already carries the control instead of flashing a screen
 * that cannot be advanced.
 */
export function useNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  const [noKeyboard, setNoKeyboard] = useState<boolean>(hasNoKeyboard);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return;
    const mql = window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY);
    const onChange = () => setNoKeyboard(mql.matches);
    onChange();
    // `addEventListener` is not universal on MediaQueryList in older Safari,
    // which is exactly the population this query is about, so the older API is
    // the fallback rather than the assumption.
    if (typeof mql.addEventListener === "function") {
      mql.addEventListener("change", onChange);
      return () => mql.removeEventListener("change", onChange);
    }
    mql.addListener?.(onChange);
    return () => mql.removeListener?.(onChange);
  }, []);

  return noKeyboard;
}

Space twice per stimulus...") left the beat and sits at the foot of the briefing, where she has as long as she likes. The foot also stops saying the timer starts the moment she presses Start, which was no longer true once the beat existed.
453:- **The check is real.** `VoiceLevel` gains `onHeard`, fired once on the first reading above the noise floor. The runner holds the count until that fires or `MIC_CHECK_SECONDS` (6) runs out, whichever is first, and the check's clock starts when the microphone opens rather than at mount, so a slow permission prompt cannot eat it. A zero-second beat has no check, which is what every existing test relies on.
454:- **Three states, three sentences.** Holding: "Say something to check your microphone." with no count showing, because a number ticking beside "say something" says the opposite. Heard: "Heard you." and "Starting in 3". Expired unheard: "Nothing heard yet. Check it is not muted." and the count. The footer status announces the one transition that matters ("Heard you. The section starts after the count.").

            {beats.map((step, i) => (
              <button
                key={i}
                ref={(el) => {

                type="button"
                role="tab"
                id={`sl-beat-tab-${i}`}
                aria-controls="sl-beat-panel"
                aria-selected={pane === "beat" && beat === i}
                tabIndex={beat === i ? 0 : -1}
                className="sl-beat rd-step"
                data-kind={STEP_KINDS[i] ?? "read"}
                onClick={() => {

                <StepScene kind={STEP_KINDS[i] ?? "read"} />
                <span className="rd-step-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <span className="sl-l">{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bl-${i}`)}</span>

      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
          It runs in one take, all the way through, out loud. You can pause: the text
          hides and the clock stops while you are away, and you cannot go back to a
          stimulus. Twenty-five minutes for the whole section is the goal you are
          working toward, not a limit.
        </p>
        {/* The key that runs the drill, said here rather than in the ready beat,
            where it was thirty words to read in three seconds. Here she has as
            long as she likes. */}
        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
          Space twice per stimulus: once when you have read it all, once when you have
          given The Recall.
        </p>
        {briefing?.closer && (
          <p className="sl-bp-closer">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.closer, "sl-bp-closer")}</p>
        )}
        {/* Says what happens to her, not how the clock works. "Counting up"
            described the mechanism and left her wondering what it counted
            toward. */}
        <p>
          When you are ready, press Start. Recording begins the moment you do. Say something so
          you can see the microphone is live, and the timer starts a few seconds after that. The
          first time, your browser will ask to use your microphone. Allow it.
        </p>
        <div className="sl-cta">{startButton("Start the section")}</div>
      </footer>
    </div>
  );

=== nokeyboard test
import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionLaunch } from "./SectionLaunch";
import { NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY } from "../recall/noKeyboard";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";

/*
 * Nothing before the run said a keyboard was needed.
 *
 * The briefing, the door and the library all render fine at 390x844, so the
 * first thing that told her a phone could not do this was the drill failing to
 * advance -- by which point the recording was already running and the run was
 * already spoiled.
 *
 * Joshua's call, 2026-09-04: warn and let her through. So this asserts the
 * warning appears AND that it takes nothing away. A notice that quietly
 * disabled Start would be the refuse-at-the-door answer he did not pick.
 */

const instructions: ActivityInstructions = {
  tagline: "One section, out loud, in one take.",
} as ActivityInstructions;

function setPointer(coarse: boolean) {
  vi.stubGlobal(
    "matchMedia",
    (query: string) =>
      ({
        matches: query === NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY ? coarse : false,
        media: query,
        onchange: null,
        addListener: () => {},
        removeListener: () => {},
        addEventListener: () => {},
        removeEventListener: () => {},
        dispatchEvent: () => false,
      }) as unknown as MediaQueryList,
  );
}

function renderDoor() {
  return render(
    <SectionLaunch
      title="PT134 Section 3"
      subtitle={null}
      instructions={instructions}
      itemCount={26}
      timeGoalMinutes={25}
      sourceSection="PT134.S3"
      studentId={null}
      onStart={() => {}}
    />,
  );
}

afterEach(() => {
  vi.unstubAllGlobals();
});

describe("the door on a device with no keyboard", () => {
  it("says what the run needs before she starts it", () => {
    setPointer(true);
    renderDoor();
    expect(screen.getByText(/built for a laptop/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("warns without refusing: Start is still there and still enabled", () => {
    setPointer(true);
    renderDoor();
    const start = screen.getByRole("button", { name: /start the section/i });
    expect(start).toBeEnabled();
  });

  it("says nothing at all where there is a keyboard", () => {
    setPointer(false);
    renderDoor();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/built for a laptop/i)).toBeNull();
    expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /start the section/i })).toBeEnabled();
  });
});

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionBriefingPage } from "./SectionBriefingPage";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import sectionInstructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The instructions page, and the three ways it went wrong.
 *
 * It arrived without any of the guards the four older surfaces carry, which is
 * exactly how an em dash and a miscounted unit reached a student.
 */
function renderPage(props?: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionBriefingPage>[0]>) {
  return render(
    <SectionBriefingPage
      title="Read, Recall, Colour · PT1 Section 3"
      subtitle="A whole section, out loud, against the clock"
      instructions={sectionInstructions as ActivityInstructions}
      onStart={vi.fn()}
      {...props}
    />,
  );
}

export function MeadowScene({ progress, children }: MeadowSceneProps) {
  const reduced = useReducedMotion();
  const rootRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  // Nothing here is worth animating while the hero is off screen.

  const ground = useMemo(() => ridge(51, 862, 12), []);

  const bladeGroups = useMemo(() => {
    const groups: Blade[][] = SWAY.map(() => []);
    for (const b of blades) groups[Math.min(SWAY.length - 1, Math.floor((b.delay / 4) * SWAY.length))].push(b);
    return groups;
  }, [blades]);

  // Each plane rides the scroll at its own rate. Negative is "up and away":
  // the foreground rises fastest so it crosses in front of whatever sits
  // behind it, which is the entire trick.
  const skyY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, 60]);
  const farY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -40]);
  const midY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -110]);
  const nearY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -190]);
  const stageY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -230]);
  const grassY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -420]);

  const still = reduced ? { y: 0 } : undefined;

  return (

=== DESIGN 140-180

One institution with two registers, and the seam between them is deliberate.

**The Hall** is the door. Unauthenticated, dark, and material: near-black ground, warm marble text, a bronze that carries the light, and a serif that behaves like carved lettering. Its own source calls it a museum nocturne, carved marble filmed in darkness, read by modern instruments. Its job is to make a prospective student feel they are entering somewhere, not signing up for something. It is the only place in the product where atmosphere outranks throughput.

**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom's white they collapse. The Hall's bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.

What they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.

Inside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room's paint.

**Key Characteristics:**
- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.
- Colour means something or it is absent.
- Flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows.
- Precise and quiet controls that stay out of the reader's way.
- Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are.

## Colors

Two grounds, one shared vocabulary of meaning.

### Primary

- **Workroom Ink** (`#121212`): the Workroom's near-black. It is the text colour, the fill of every primary button, and the colour of the focus ring. The Workroom has no accent, so ink carries emphasis by itself.
- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall's warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall's night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.

### Secondary

- **Hall Cyan** (`#8ad8e2`): the Hall's cool instrument note against the bronze, and the colour of its focus outlines. Rare by design; it marks precision where bronze marks warmth.
- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.

### Tertiary

Individual rooms carry their own palettes behind a wrapper class and are documented in their own surface briefs, not here. The wrappers in use are `.classroom-root`, `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk`, and the eight Activity Centre worlds.

`.classroom-root` was added to this list on 2026-09-08, after an audit found the classroom running on Outfit and a warm palette while this record still described it as neutral Workroom chrome. It is a sanctioned world, not drift: the warmth and the typeface are deliberate and they are what makes the classroom feel like a study rather than a dashboard. The record was wrong, not the code. Its brief is `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md`. Note that this is the one sanctioned world that is also a corridor, so the rule below about corridors never being worlds now has exactly one documented exception.

### Neutral

- **Workroom Page** (`#fcfcfc`) and **Card** (`#ffffff`): the page ground and the surface that sits on it. One step of separation, no more.
=== DESIGN 198-226
**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.

## Typography

**Workroom Body:** Lexend (with Outfit, then the system stack)
**Hall Display:** Fraunces (with Iowan Old Style, Times New Roman, serif)
**Hall Inscription:** Cinzel (with Trajan Pro, serif)
**Mono:** IBM Plex Mono
**Question Lock:** Outfit, held deliberately for LSAT question surfaces

**Character:** The Workroom is one humanist sans doing everything, sized and weighted rather than swapped. The Hall is a serif institution: an optical serif for its headings and an inscriptional face for its few carved lines. The split is the whole typographic idea. Inside, type gets out of the way; at the door, type is the architecture.

### Hierarchy

- **Title** (600, 24px, line-height 1, tracking -0.025em): card and section headings in the Workroom.
- **Body** (400, 14px rising to 16px at 640px and above, line-height 1.6): everything a student reads in the Workroom chrome.
- **Question** (400, 16px, line-height 1.6, Outfit): LSAT stimulus and answer choices. Font-locked to Outfit even though the rest of the app moved to Lexend, so question text does not shift when the shell's face changes.
- **Label** (500, 14px): button and control labels.
- **Hall Display** (380, fluid, tracking -0.015em, Fraunces): Hall headings. The unusually light 380 weight is the point; it reads as carved rather than printed.
- **Hall Inscription** (500, tracking 0.05em, Cinzel): the Hall's few short carved lines. Never body copy.
- **Hall Mono** (400, 13px, tracking 0.04em, IBM Plex Mono): the Hall's instrument labels.

### Named Rules

**The One Face Rule.** The Workroom uses a single family. Hierarchy is built from size, weight and space. A second family inside the Workroom is a defect unless it is the Outfit question lock.

**The Question Never Moves Rule.** LSAT question text is font-locked. A change to the shell's typeface must not reflow a passage a student is mid-way through reading.

**The Serif Stays At The Door Rule.** Fraunces and Cinzel belong to the Hall. A serif appearing in the Workroom chrome means the seam has leaked.

434:Step 7 above says to warm to `--rc-page`. The handover copies a value it cannot reference, since neither colour is in scope for the other's stylesheet, so `recall/section.css.test.ts` now holds the copy to the original.

/* Read, Recall, Colour, section mode.
 *
 * The v1 runner sits on a dark field because it shows one chunk at a time and
 * nothing during recall: there is barely anything on screen to light. A section
 * is the opposite surface. The whole stimulus is present for the whole
 * stimulus, it is the only thing on screen, and she is reading it closely for
 * twenty minutes. That is a document, and a document belongs on paper.
 *
 * So this screen wears the real section's clothes. `OfficialDrillFrame` already
 * mirrors LawHub -- indigo field, white header rail, one centred white card,
 * Fraunces at 19px -- and that is the surface she will sit on test day. Every
 * value below is taken from it rather than invented, and the answering half is
 * removed rather than restyled. No new dialect: this is the drill room.
 *
 * Everything after the frame exists to keep the text in exactly the same place
 * while its state changes.
 */

.ac-section {
  --sec-field: #1a1635;
  --sec-paper: #ffffff;
  --sec-ink: #111827;
  --sec-rule: #cbd5e1;
  --sec-chrome: #334155;
  --sec-warn: #b45309;
  --sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;

  display: flex;
  min-height: 100vh;
  flex-direction: column;

        elapsedSeconds={beat ? 0 : 754}
        /* The real run passes both, and the lab exists to be looked at: without
           them the heading and the goal beside the clock are the two things on
           this surface nobody can see here. */
        sectionName={run.sourceSection ?? undefined}
        goalSeconds={run.timeGoalMinutes.standard * 60}
        countdown={beat ? 3 : null}
        paused={paused}
        onPause={beat ? undefined : () => setMoment("paused")}
        onResume={() => setMoment("running")}
        // No microphone in the lab, so the bar mounts and stays flat. That is
        // exactly what a student with a dead input sees, which is the reason
        // the indicator exists and the one state worth being able to look at.
        getStream={() => null}
        onExit={() => setConfirming(true)}
        confirmingExit={confirming}
        onConfirmExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
        onCancelExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
      />
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

=== brief 45-100
## Read, Recall, Colour door (locked 2026-09-05)

The two RRC doors (`SectionLibraryLaunch`, `SectionLaunch`, both drawn by `SectionLaunchFrame`) are a committed world of their own, mode **Persuade** at the top and **Operate** in the slab. Joshua's brief: a layered, high-end landing page in the spirit of heylemon.ai (cream ground, painted panels, serif display with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, a dictation demo) with fora.so's depth-plane hero, and bold step 1 to 4 instructions.

- **World:** one dusk. The runner's indigo (`#1a1635`) lifted into a sky over a painted meadow, cream paper (`#f6f3ea`) beneath. Display is Fraunces at opsz 144; body Inter; labels IBM Plex Mono. Marigold marks the Colour step, emerald marks a carried detail, nothing else is coloured.
- **Hero:** `MeadowScene` paints five depth planes generatively in SVG (seeded ridges, seeded grass blades, feTurbulence grain) so no raster ships; each plane rides `useScroll` at its own rate and the foreground grass crosses in front of the demo card. The headline blurs and recedes on the same scroll value.
- **Signature moment:** `RecallDemo`, a looping performance of the method on a synthetic stimulus, labelled as such, whose text literally dissolves at the Recall step.
- **Steps:** four cards, each with a small drawn scene, doubling as the ARIA tablist the tests already contract (`sl-beat`, `sl-beat-tab-N`, `sl-beat-panel`). The full briefing is the second tab of the drawer.
- **Tokens:** scoped to `.sl` as `--rd-*`. The design detector reports them as outside DESIGN.md; that is the sanctioned-world exception above, not drift.

## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)

Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream, not
emerald, and not grey** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI
looking", "I don't want emerald", "it should be much more colorful"). The second
sanctioned departure from the wing's surface after the RRC door.

- **Two grounds.** A deep plum console (`oklch(0.245 0.058 312)`) holds the
  verdict, the transport and the ledger; a violet-cast white page
  (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`) holds everything she reads at length.
- **Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity.** The mechanic is
  Grammarly's, which colours by category and reserves red for outright errors.
  Four fixed tones: **Detail** plum-rose, **Read** teal, **Colour** gold,
  **Method** indigo. A miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of, which is
  what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure.
- **Signal magenta is sound, not a category.** It marks the line currently
  playing and nothing else, held at hue 328 so it separates from the
  plum-rose dropped chips it shares a line with.
- **Hues chosen from research, not taste** (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). Saturated
  right now: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and
  terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft
  cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. Green and cream are the
  owner's veto; orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used
  list. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is
  (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary), a system built to hold AA.
- **Band is fill, not hue.** The ledger's marks differ by how full they are, in
  gold; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow rose ring. It survives
  grayscale, which is what lets category own the colour.
- **One stimulus at a time.** Twenty-two accordion rows became a ledger of
  twenty-two marks plus one open stimulus in a fixed order: the passage, what
  you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.
- **No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and she has
  never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says
  "Almost all of it came back" instead (Joshua, 2026-09-08).
- **Contrast is measured, not assumed.** `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs` converts
  computed colours through a canvas (Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`) and
  every text node clears 4.5:1 on its own ground, in the default, playing and
  clean states.
- **Type ramp:** Fraunces display, Lexend body, IBM Plex Mono labels. The design
  detector reports the sizes as outside DESIGN.md; that is this sanctioned world,
  not drift, the same exception the RRC door's `--rd-*` tokens carry.
- **Harness:** `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders it at twenty-two
  stimuli without `App.tsx`. `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to
  freeze the playhead on a line. `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs` captures the matrix.

=== recall css test
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

/**
 * The handover warms to the ground the card actually lands on.
 *
 * `.ac-section.is-finishing` copies a colour it cannot reference: the report
 * phase's ground is declared in `report-card.css`, scoped to elements the
 * runner is not inside. A copied value drifts silently, and this one already
 * had: the field warmed to the Activity Centre's cream while the card arrived
 * on a violet ground, so the ten seconds after twenty-five unbroken minutes
 * crossed three grounds instead of one.
 */
const HERE = __dirname;
const SECTION = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, "section.css"), "utf8");
const REPORT_CARD = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, "../components/report-card.css"), "utf8");

/** The `background:` of the first rule whose selector list contains `selector`. */
function backgroundOf(css: string, selector: string): string {
  const at = css.indexOf(selector);
  const open = css.indexOf("{", at);
  const body = css.slice(open, css.indexOf("}", open));
  return (body.match(/background:\s*([^;]+);/) ?? [])[1]?.trim() ?? "";
}

describe("the handover out of the drill room", () => {
  it("warms to the same colour the report phase paints", () => {
    const warmsTo = backgroundOf(SECTION, ".ac-section.is-finishing");
    const landsOn = backgroundOf(REPORT_CARD, ".rc-ground");
    expect(warmsTo).not.toBe("");
    expect(warmsTo).toBe(landsOn);
  });

  it("does not warm to the Activity Centre's cream, which nothing downstream paints", () => {
    expect(backgroundOf(SECTION, ".ac-section.is-finishing")).not.toMatch(/38\s+36%\s+93%/);
  });
});

export default function ActivityLab() {
  const [world, setWorld] = useState<World>("switchyard");

  return (
    <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen">
      <header className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 overflow-hidden border-b border-border px-6 py-4">
        <div className="mr-auto">
          <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
            Activity Lab · dev only
          </p>
          <h1 className="ac-display text-xl text-foreground">
            {WORLDS.find((w) => w.key === world)!.label}
          </h1>
        </div>
        <Tabs
          options={WORLDS.map((w) => w.key)}
          value={world}
          onChange={(v) => setWorld(v)}
          label="World"
        />
      </header>

      {world === "list" ? <ListPanel /> : null}
      {world === "intro" ? <IntroPanel /> : null}
      {world === "door" ? <DoorPanel /> : null}
      {world === "section" ? <SectionPanel /> : null}
      {world === "report" ? <ReportPanel /> : null}
